- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Maria I, leader of Portugal (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Portugal
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Nau, Replacing Caravel (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Caçador, Replacing Gatling Gun (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: University of Coimbra, Replacing Imperial College (Requires technology: Education)
        - Unique Improvement: Feitoria (Can be constructed by Workers in owned land to provide yields or by Portuguese-owned Naus (via Sell Exotic Goods) in City-State land.
          In owned land:
          Can only be built on coastal tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Feitoria.
          In City-State land:
          Provides an untradeable copy of each Luxury Resource type owned by the City-State. Trade Routes to this City-State generate bonus Food and Production based on Gold generated from the Trade Route and relationship with the City-State (i.e. Neutral/Friend/Ally).
          In addition to these bonuses, it provides vision within a 2-tile radius and a +25% Defensive Strength bonus for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains a Canal, allowing friendly Sea Units to traverse through this tile.); Requires technology: Compass
        - Civilization Ability: For each active Trade Route, receive +4 Gold, Science, and Great Admiral () or Great General () Points, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Maria I
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Ballistics: Allows you to build Anti-Aircraft Guns, useful for thwarting enemy aircraft, and the Machine Gun, a powerful single-hex ranged Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Rocketry, Combined Arms
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      
      ## Policies
      - Empiricism (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * +1 Food for every Citizen and +25% Growth in all Cities.
        - * +3% Science in a City for every Great Work present (up to 20%).
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Economic Union (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Economic Union: Receive two additional Trade Routes. +6 Gold from trade routes with civilizations following Freedom.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        - Rationale: India 97% to victory in 2 turns. Zero chattiness to avoid any diplomatic delays. Maximum aggression maintained. No deception - enemies know I'm coming. Total focus on war declaration and strike execution before turn 354 when India becomes Influential.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None

        ## Relationships
        
        ### Arabia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Arabia reduced to vassal status under Iroquois, only Damascus remains, and peace treaty is active. Reset relationship to neutral - they're no longer a threat and our ally The Iroquois controls them. No need to maintain hostility.
        - UpdatedTurn: 239
        
        ### Siam
        - Public: 30
        - Private: 30
        - Rationale: Siam remains friendly with us and fighting Rome. They have 3575 military strength and a defensive pact with The Iroquois. With The Iroquois about to fall under Indian influence, Siam is our secondary line of defense. They have Open Borders with India, but maintaining positive relations keeps them from actively joining an anti-Portuguese coalition. Their military strength combined with ours provides deterrence.
        - UpdatedTurn: 325
        
        ### Byzantium
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Byzantium has Defensive Pact with India and is under Indian cultural influence. They are a direct threat that could attack when I DOW India. Maintaining negative relations to prepare for potential betrayal. Cannot trust them.
        - UpdatedTurn: 344
        
        ### Carthage
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Carthage is defeated with only Hippo Regius remaining. Reset to neutral to prepare for peace treaty and free up diplomatic focus for India war. No need to maintain hostility against defeated foe.
        - UpdatedTurn: 352
        
        ### India
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: INDIA: 97% POPULAR, 2 TURNS FROM INFLUENTIAL CULTURAL VICTORY. Total war is now the only option. Must declare war immediately and attempt nuclear strike or conventional assault with paratroopers/bombers. Maintaining -100/-100 relations to prepare for immediate DOW.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: War with Rome is now a distraction from the existential India threat. Resetting to neutral to free forces and prepare for potential last-ditch strike against India, though they will likely win cultural victory next turn regardless.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: 90
        - Private: 90
        - Rationale: The Iroquois: Now have Open Borders with India and are under Indian cultural influence (Influential status). They could flip to India's side when war starts. Maintaining 90/90 to delay betrayal. If they join India, I'm surrounded by enemies. The defensive pact is the only thing keeping them neutral.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Atomic Theory research continuing (14 turns). No policy available. Maintaining current strategic posture as India will win cultural victory next turn regardless of actions. Maximum war readiness in case of miracle.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 294
          - Open Door: Ur:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: 328
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: 294
          - World Religion: Hinduism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Rome:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 278
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - Proposer: India
          - Enact: United Nations:
            - Description: Begins the United Nations project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : United Nations wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World. Also, when you expend a Great Person, earn 25 Influence with all City-States you have met. Triggers a Golden Age, as well as the United Nations Special Session of the World Congress if the world has reached the Atomic Era.)
              : Receive an immediate boost of 25 Influence with all City-States. The Consulate National Wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.)
              : Receive a free Great Diplomat.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - Proposer: Siam
          - Enact: Byzantine Sphere of Influence: Singapore:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: Byzantium

        You, Maria I (leader of Portugal, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 354.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Maria I, leader of Portugal (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Portugal
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Nau, Replacing Caravel (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Caçador, Replacing Gatling Gun (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: University of Coimbra, Replacing Imperial College (Requires technology: Education)
        - Unique Improvement: Feitoria (Can be constructed by Workers in owned land to provide yields or by Portuguese-owned Naus (via Sell Exotic Goods) in City-State land.
          In owned land:
          Can only be built on coastal tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Feitoria.
          In City-State land:
          Provides an untradeable copy of each Luxury Resource type owned by the City-State. Trade Routes to this City-State generate bonus Food and Production based on Gold generated from the Trade Route and relationship with the City-State (i.e. Neutral/Friend/Ally).
          In addition to these bonuses, it provides vision within a 2-tile radius and a +25% Defensive Strength bonus for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains a Canal, allowing friendly Sea Units to traverse through this tile.); Requires technology: Compass
        - Civilization Ability: For each active Trade Route, receive +4 Gold, Science, and Great Admiral () or Great General () Points, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Maria I
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Ballistics: Allows you to build Anti-Aircraft Guns, useful for thwarting enemy aircraft, and the Machine Gun, a powerful single-hex ranged Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Rocketry, Combined Arms
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      
      ## Policies
      - Empiricism (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * +1 Food for every Citizen and +25% Growth in all Cities.
        - * +3% Science in a City for every Great Work present (up to 20%).
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Economic Union (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Economic Union: Receive two additional Trade Routes. +6 Gold from trade routes with civilizations following Freedom.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Opponents lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

We lower Meanness and WarBias, and increase Forgiveness and DiplomaticBalance, to make our staff receptive to peace offers from Rome.     - #1:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Private: 40
        - Public: 40
        - Rationale: We must preserve Rome as a cultural buffer. If Rome is eliminated, India (which is influential over all other civs) wins an instant Cultural Victory. Setting relations to positive to encourage our staff to make peace and stop destroying Rome's culture.
        - TargetID: 1
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Private: 40
        - TargetID: 4
        - Rationale: Byzantium has a high opinion of us (+70) and does not have a Defensive Pact with India. Positive relations secure our flank, protect trade, and prevent unnecessary conflict.
        - Public: 40
    - #3:

      - args:
        - TargetID: 5
        - Rationale: Siam is a friendly, fellow Freedom ideology civ with a Defensive Pact with our ally The Iroquois. Solidifying this relationship strengthens our ideological bloc against India.

        - Private: 50

        - Rationale: Empiricism is the final policy in the Rationalism branch. Completing Rationalism will grant us a massive scientific boost, crucial for rushing towards nuclear technologies.

We pivot to a consolidative and defensive posture, focusing on Science, Growth, Gold, and Infrastructure, while scaling back offensive military operations to allow our empire to recover and rebuild.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Analyzing Victory Progress**
            
            My current focus is on assessing our Victory Progress, specifically whether India has secured a win. It's Turn 354 now. In the preceding turn, India was at 97% popularity and nearing cultural influence within two turns.
            
            **Confirming Victory Status**
            
            My attention is now on confirming India's victory. It seems they haven't yet achieved cultural influence over Rome, which is still 41 turns away. This means we have a crucial window of opportunity.
            
            **Assessing Strategic Position**
            
            I'm currently re-evaluating our strategic position, realizing our war with Carthage is nearly concluded, with only two cities remaining for them. This complication with Rome, as Carthage's master, necessitates a renewed focus on our military actions and immediate goals.
            
            **Revising Alliance Strategy**
            
            I'm revising our alliance strategy, noting Rome's proximity and the Iroquois' simultaneous war with them. India recently bought their freedom from the Iroquois, which complicates our diplomatic landscape and requires a fresh look at our current military commitments.
            
            **Analyzing Resource Gains**
            
            I've just processed a significant influx of 5133 gold from a trade with the Iroquois, though it seems our current balance is only 774. This discrepancy warrants investigation, especially given recent unit upgrades and city training using gold.
            
            **Acquiring Military Assets**
            
            I've just observed a substantial investment in bombers and triplanes, funded by our recent gold influx. This suggests a proactive shift towards offensive capabilities, potentially to address evolving strategic threats or seize opportunities.
            
            **Evaluating Grand Strategy**
            
            I am now scrutinizing our Grand Strategy, questioning if Conquest remains optimal given Portugal's control of key capitals like Lisbon, Mecca, and Carthage. Despite our ongoing wars and military strength, India's dominance poses a significant challenge, prompting a review of our current approach and potential shifts in focus.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Maneuvers**
            
            I'm currently assessing the complex web of alliances and rivalries, noting India's wide-reaching open borders except with Rome, who has denounced them. My own denouncement of India, coupled with friendships with the Iroquois, Siam, and Byzantium, creates a challenging diplomatic landscape.
            
            **Revising Diplomatic Standing**
            
            I'm currently reassessing my perception of Byzantium. Despite my previous concerns and setting our relationship to a negative value due to their ties with India and potential threat, their public opinion of me is surprisingly high, at +70. This positive sentiment, coupled with our shared Declarations of Friendship and trade partnership, warrants re-evaluation of our past caution.
            
            **Clarifying Defensive Pacts**
            
            I'm double-checking defensive pacts to confirm that declaring war on India won't trigger alliances against us, as it appears they have none. This is a crucial detail for our next move.
            
            **Considering War Declarations**
            
            I'm currently questioning the wisdom of declaring war on India, especially since they're still 41 turns from a cultural victory. Their military strength is formidable, far exceeding our combined forces. I'm now contemplating if focusing on Rome and Carthage first, then building up our own strength, is a more viable path.
            
            **Researching Next Steps**
            
            I am currently analyzing our research path. With Atomic Theory nearing completion, I'm evaluating the next available technologies: Ballistics, Refrigeration, and Penicillin. I'm trying to understand why Nuclear Fission isn't an immediate option and if prerequisite technologies are required.
            
            **Researching Ballistics**
            
            I'm currently prioritizing research, specifically investigating the prerequisites for Nuclear Fission. It seems Ballistics is the most strategic choice now, as it unlocks crucial military advancements like Rocketry and Combined Arms, aligning with our Conquest victory goal.
            
            **Adopting Policy Branch**
            
            I am currently evaluating our policy choices. Having significantly invested in the Rationalism branch, completing it with Empiricism appears to offer substantial bonuses and strategic advantages. I need to confirm the remaining options within this branch before finalizing our next adoption.
            
            **Adopting Policy Empiricism**
            
            I've confirmed that Empiricism is the final policy in the Rationalism branch. Completing this branch will provide a significant science boost, which is essential for developing advanced military units to counter India's formidable army and achieve our Conquest victory.
            
            **Assessing Diplomatic Reactions**
            
            I'm currently reviewing the repercussions of declaring war on India, specifically concerning our alliances with Siam, Byzantium, and the Iroquois. It appears Byzantium, being friends with India, might react negatively, whereas Siam and the Iroquois, who have open borders but no formal pacts with India, should remain neutral. However, India's considerable military strength versus our own remains a significant deterrent, prompting a re-evaluation of the immediate benefits versus the risks.
            
            **Assessing Troop Movements**
            
            I'm now analyzing how India might deploy their massive army. Given their open borders with Siam, Byzantium, Iroquois, and Arabia, they could march directly to our cities. This, coupled with their current peace and immense military strength, presents a significant and immediate threat that demands careful consideration.
            
            **Crushing Remaining Foes**
            
            I'm now focused on finalizing our wars with Rome and Carthage. Our combined strength with the Iroquois far surpasses theirs, and logs confirm we are steamrolling their territories. This victory will significantly boost our resources and position before considering any further major conflicts.
            
            **Bombarding Coastal Cities**
            
            My focus has shifted to exploiting our naval superiority to capture remaining enemy cities. Our powerful navy can easily bombard and reduce the defenses of coastal targets like Gades and Hippo Regius, paving the way for their swift capture and the complete elimination of Carthage.
            
            **Conquering Roman Capitals**
            
            I'm now realizing Rome possesses multiple capitals, including Antium and Rome itself, alongside other significant cities. Their military is considerably weaker than ours, especially since they are simultaneously engaged with us and the Iroquois. This presents a clear opportunity to seize their remaining capitals and expand our empire significantly, adding to Lisbon, Mecca, and Carthage. I need to confirm if Damascus, Arabia's capital, is also a target or if Mecca was their original capital.
            
            **Preventing Cultural Victory**
            
            I've just realized that eliminating Rome would inadvertently accelerate India's cultural victory by reducing the number of major civilizations to six, which India already dominates. This means I must halt our conquest of Rome immediately to prevent this outcome and reconsider our immediate strategic objectives.
            
            **Halting Offensive Actions**
            
            I've just had a critical realization: eliminating Rome would instantly trigger India's cultural victory. I must immediately halt our offensive against them to prevent this, as India is influential over all other civilizations except Rome. We need to keep Rome alive at all costs to maintain our game.
            
            **Halting Offensive Actions**
            
            I've just had a critical realization: eliminating Rome would instantly trigger India's cultural victory. I must immediately halt our offensive against them to prevent this, as India is influential over all other civilizations except Rome. We need to keep Rome alive at all costs to maintain our game.
            
            **Maintaining Roman Cities**
            
            My current priority is ensuring Rome retains at least one city. Capturing their cities is acceptable, but their complete elimination is a direct path to India's cultural victory. We must actively manage this to prevent accelerating India's win condition.
            
            **Balancing Expansion**
            
            I'm re-evaluating the impact of weakening Rome. While reducing their cities boosts our expansion, it also accelerates India's cultural influence over them, bringing their victory closer. This creates a delicate balance between territorial gains and preventing India's win.
            
            **Assessing Cultural Impact**
            
            I'm analyzing how weakening Rome directly aids India's cultural victory. By reducing Rome's culture output, we inadvertently increase India's lifetime tourism-to-culture ratio, significantly shortening their path to influence. This is a crucial dynamic to manage.
            
            **Developing a Counter-Strategy**
            
            I am now contemplating methods to counter India's cultural victory. Options include increasing our own culture, declaring war on India to disrupt their tourism, capturing their cities, or supporting Rome's defenses to slow India's influence.
            
            **Researching Strategic Options**
            
            I am currently investigating strategic paths to prevent India's cultural victory. This involves a thorough assessment of options, from boosting our own cultural output to directly engaging India militarily and disrupting their tourism generation.
            
            **Evaluating Intervention Tactics**
            
            I am now focused on evaluating direct intervention strategies against India. This includes assessing the feasibility and impact of declaring war and pillaging their tourism-generating improvements, a potentially decisive move.
            
            **Considering Offensive Actions**
            
            I'm now evaluating the possibility of launching a direct offensive against India. This includes analyzing the potential benefits of capturing their cities and disrupting their tourism infrastructure to thwart their cultural victory.
            
            **Bolstering Defensive Capabilities**
            
            I am currently considering how to best support Rome's defense. Strengthening their ability to resist India's cultural influence is a key strategy in preventing an immediate loss for us.
            
            **Defending Against Cultural Victory**
            
            I'm now focusing on preventing India's cultural victory. Rome's higher culture per turn is slowing them, but capturing Rome's cities will reduce their culture, accelerating India's win. I need to explore making peace with Rome and potentially even assisting them to maintain their cultural output, as our priority is stopping India.
            
            **Bribing Civilizations**
            
            I'm now investigating the possibility of bribing other civilizations to declare war on India. With a substantial gold reserve, I'm looking to leverage this to disrupt India's dominance. I'm checking which civs have open borders with India but no defensive pacts, specifically Siam and the Iroquois, to see if they can be convinced to attack India, given Siam's defensive pact with Byzantium is a complicating factor.
            
            **Negotiating Peace Offers**
            
            I am now assessing the viability of peace negotiations with Rome and Carthage. My primary concern is maintaining Rome's cultural output as a bulwark against India's victory. I will also explore setting friendlier relationships to encourage peace, leveraging our gold reserves to potentially bribe Siam and the Iroquois into attacking India.
            
            **Stopping City Razing**
            
            I've realized that continuing to raze Utique is counterproductive given the current strategic situation. I need to cease this action immediately to preserve infrastructure and prevent further unhappiness, and then focus on consolidating our captured territories while reassessing our diplomatic and military objectives.
            
            **Leveraging Diplomatic Insight**
            
            I'm now leveraging new diplomatic intel. Byzantium, contrary to earlier assumptions, does not have a defensive pact with India, only a Declaration of Friendship. Their positive sentiment towards us and shared partnerships provide a surprising opening to reassess our past caution.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Relationships**
            
            I'm now fine-tuning our diplomatic standing with Byzantium and Rome. I'm setting Byzantium to a friendly stance to secure our flank and maintain trade, given their high opinion of us and lack of pacts with India. For Rome, maintaining their cities is key to preventing India's cultural win.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Approach**
            
            I'm now refining our diplomatic approach towards Rome. Recognizing that their cultural output is our key defense against India's impending victory, I'm actively seeking peace to allow them to recover and reinforce their cultural influence. This also informs my decision to foster positive relations with Byzantium, ensuring a stable flank.
            
            **Strengthening Global Alliances**
            
            I'm now focusing on solidifying alliances, particularly with Siam and the Iroquois, to bolster our position against India. I need to confirm if I can interact with multiple tools simultaneously for efficiency, and I'm reviewing our current aggressive persona to see if adjustments are needed in light of these diplomatic efforts.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Persona**
            
            I'm now reassessing our AI's core diplomatic persona. Given our need to broker peace with Rome and leverage alliances, I recognize that high Meanness and WarBias values are counterproductive. I need to adjust these parameters to allow for peaceful resolutions, particularly with Rome, while still maintaining our competitive edge and readiness for future conflicts.
            
            **Balancing Diplomatic Persona**
            
            I'm now fine-tuning our diplomatic persona, lowering Meanness and WarBias while increasing Forgiveness and DiplomaticBalance. This aims to facilitate peace with Rome, essential for stalling India's cultural victory, and strengthen alliances, setting the stage for our eventual confrontation.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Persona**
            
            I'm now fine-tuning our diplomatic persona, lowering Meanness and WarBias while increasing Forgiveness and DiplomaticBalance. This aims to facilitate peace with Rome, essential for stalling India's cultural victory, and strengthen alliances, setting the stage for our eventual confrontation.
            
            **Adjusting Civilization Flavors**
            
            I'm now reallocating our civilization's core flavors. With a focus on accelerating scientific research, especially for Atomic Theory and Nuclear Fission, Science has been significantly boosted. Production remains high for infrastructure and units, while Gold is increased for military upkeep and upgrades. Culture and Growth are also being elevated to counter future threats and develop our cities, with Espionage remaining at peak to protect our technological advancements. Offense is reduced as we shift to a more defensive posture, bolstered by increased Defense and Mobilization, and a stronger emphasis on Infrastructure and Tile Improvements.
            
            **Refining Civilization Focus**
            
            I am now reallocating our civilization's core flavors. I've significantly boosted Science to accelerate research like Atomic Theory, while keeping Production high for infrastructure and units. Gold is increased for military upkeep, and Culture and Growth are elevated for development. Offense is reduced, with increased Defense and Mobilization to protect against India's bombers. Espionage remains high to safeguard our advancements.
            
            **Shifting Grand Strategy**
            
            I am now re-evaluating our overall strategy. While Conquest has served us, I'm considering a shift to the Spaceship victory type. This would allow me to prioritize science and infrastructure, crucial for unlocking Nuclear Fission and securing a different path to victory, potentially while we focus on diplomacy and defense.
            
            **Shifting Strategy to Spaceship**
            
            I am now re-evaluating our overall strategy. While Conquest has served us, I'm considering a shift to the Spaceship victory type. This would allow me to prioritize science and infrastructure, crucial for unlocking Nuclear Fission and securing a different path to victory, potentially while we focus on diplomacy and defense.
            
            **Adjusting Relationships**
            
            I am now setting positive diplomatic relationships with Rome and Byzantium. For Rome, this is to encourage peace and preserve their culture as a buffer against India's victory. For Byzantium, it's to leverage their high opinion of us and lack of pacts with India.
            
            **Refining Diplomatic Interactions**
            
            I'm now focusing on adjusting our diplomatic persona. Lowering Meanness and WarBias, while increasing Forgiveness and DiplomaticBalance, will help facilitate peace with Rome and strengthen alliances against India. This aims to secure our long-term victory path.
            
            **Focusing on Spaceship Victory**
            
            I am now shifting our grand strategy towards a Spaceship victory. This involves prioritizing Science and Espionage research to accelerate technological advancement, alongside robust Production and Defense to build necessary infrastructure and protect our borders.
